  ⚠ **`netServerChordMax` SCALES AND THE DEFAULT BAND IS UNCHANGED.** A flat budget of two is a sensible fraction of a five-server world and nothing on an eighteen-server one, so the cross-link setting would have been invisible at the sizes players raise it for. `max(2, servers/3)` leaves 5–7 on exactly 2 and gives 18 six. ⚠ **No setting can disconnect the world** — the spanning tree is built first and never removed, so connectivity is a property of the construction; tested at 0% cross-links.
  ⚠ **THE TAB STRIP HAD TO WRAP.** It was an `HBox`, which lays out on one line whatever the width — a dozen servers pushed tabs off the edge with nothing to scroll and no sign they existed, the map silently losing the only control that reaches half the world. A `FlowPane` now; rendered at 18 servers, two rows.
  ⚠ **`BalanceTest` and `SweepDeterminismTest` both moved, and only one was a literal.** The first pinned `NET_SERVERS_MAX == 7`. The second asserted a Topology Mapper sweep finds a superset of the narrower one, which **the rules do not guarantee** — see §2 **NET-2**; it was passing on a lucky seed and is now asserted at the property that holds.

- 2026-08-09 — **`DEPTH n FROM HOME` described the server you were standing on, not the tab you had open** — `ServerTabs.filter`, `ServerTabsTest.CurrentServer`. The map's header reads `SERVER <name> DEPTH n FROM HOME` off `currentServer()`, and a filtered map kept the **vantage's** server there — so opening a server four bridges out still read `DEPTH 0 FROM HOME` under the name of the server the player had just navigated away from. ⚠ The depth is the one number on that strip whose whole job is to say how dangerous this place is, and `design/18` §4 makes the difficulty gradient key on it. ⚠ **Nothing is lost by re-pointing it**: where the player is *standing* is carried separately as `vantageAddress`, which the same strip prints as `SWEEPING FROM` and which the graph marks with the heavy frame — on a filtered map "current server" can only sensibly mean the one on screen. ⚠ `ServerTabs.of` must therefore keep being given the **unfiltered** world or every tab reports itself as current; asserted. ⚠ Verified by rendering `subtle-kotake` at `DEPTH 3 FROM HOME` while sweeping from `10.0.0.1`, and negative-tested.

- 2026-08-09 — **Every world has a bridge, a bridge is named for where it goes, and a server reaches the tab strip when its bridge is BREACHED** — on explicit direction. [`18-network-topology.md`](18-network-topology.md) §2.7–2.8. `HostState.operator`, `NpcNames.bridgeOperator`, `TopologyGenerator.{nameBridgeOperators,relabelLegacy}`, `VirtualFs.hostUser`, `NetRules.{view,revealAll}`; `BridgeAndServerTabsTest`, `DeckSnapshot -Ddeck.reveal=1`.

  ⚠ **THE "AT LEAST ONE BRIDGE" GUARANTEE ALREADY HELD, BY CONSTRUCTION, AND NOTHING SAID SO.** `NET_SERVERS_MIN` is 5 and the spanning tree attaches every server to one already placed, so home always has an edge and step 5 turns every edge into a pair of bridges. Pinned over 400 seeds rather than added — the honest report is that this half of the request was already true, and the two edits that would break it would have failed no other test.
  ⚠ **A BRIDGE'S ACCOUNT COMES FROM `CHARACTERS`, matching the far server's name** — `muaddib@…` is a door to `<adjective>-muaddib`. It turns the identity finding into a second, quieter way of learning what is on the other side of a door. ⚠ **Stored on `HostState`, not derived**, and that is the one exception in the naming scheme: it is a fact about *two* machines on two servers, so it cannot come from this host's address, and `VirtualFs` is deliberately a pure function of one host. Storing one string beat threading the topology into the filesystem, the shell, the file manager and the scanner. ⚠ **Symmetric** — both ends of a link are doors, and naming only the home end leaves the machine met *after* crossing looking ordinary. ⚠ **Zero draws**, so every existing world regenerates identically; ⚠ **after `applyHomeFloor`**, which may promote a different machine to bridge, or the map advertises the wrong door. ⚠ **It can exceed the seven-character operator budget and the box clips it** — the treatment machine names already get there, with the full string on the tooltip, in the host list and in the recon file.
  ⚠ **TABS NOW NEED A BREACH, AND BEFORE THIS THEY NEEDED THREE MORE ACTIONS.** `knownServers` came from sightings alone, so a foreign server appeared only once a machine on it had been swept — foothold, `connect`, *then* sweep. §2.6's own note about a tab that "carries the name and says it is unexplored" described a state that could not occur; it can now. ⚠ **Breached, not identified**: a tab is a place to go, and identification would put one on the strip for a door the player cannot open. ⚠ **The discovery half still binds**, so a foothold on a bridge nobody has found publishes nothing.
  ⚠ **The developer reveal breaches every bridge**, or a "reveal the whole map" leaves every tab but home missing. **Bridges only**, and it is a real capability grant rather than a display change — a foothold is what `connect` checks. Nothing gets `looted`. ⚠ Granted **outside** the already-discovered guard, or revealing after exploring opens fewer bridges than revealing first.
  ⚠ **Verified by rendering both states**: an unrevealed map four repositions in shows **one** tab; a revealed one shows all **seven**. And negative-tested — removing the tab rule fires `breachingIsEnough`, removing the account pass fires two more.

- 2026-08-09 — **A developer/cheat facility, revealed by a key sequence in Settings — SOLO ONLY** — on explicit direction. `engine/state/CheatState`, `engine/rules/Cheats`, `client/session/CheatFacility`, `client/view/CheatsView`, `client/ui/SecretCode`; `CheatsTest`, `CheatFacilityTest`, `SecretCodeTest`. Sliders for the compute ceiling and personal heat, ethecoin grants, switches for thermal recovery / heat freeze / pre-solved breaches, a scale on the counter-hack chance, a manual intrusion, and a whole-map reveal. Entered as `↑ ↓ ← → Shift+A Shift+B Enter` while the Settings window has focus.

  ⚠ **IT PLAINLY STEPS OVER I1, I2 AND I5, AND THE ARGUMENT FOR ALLOWING IT IS NARROW.** Every invariant these cheats walk past exists to keep a **shared** economy honest — they stop one player buying a ceiling another had to earn, and stop a market being flooded with money nobody mined. None of that is in play on a character that cannot reach anybody. ⚠ **The whole safety argument is therefore the solo gate, not the cheat code**, and it is mechanical in three independent places: the facility is **absent from the `GameSession` port** (`GameEngine.rename`'s precedent — "the honest way to make something impossible is for it to be absent"), `CheatFacility.forSession` answers empty for anything whose `mode()` is not `SOLO`, and the rules tier refuses on `GameSave.federable` on its own account. ⚠ **Do not make cheats a wire operation.**
  ⚠ **A SOLO SAVE WAS ALWAYS THIS EDITABLE** — CLAUDE.md's own I14 note says a local H2 file is "exactly as editable as JSON was, merely less pleasant to read". What changed is that the game now provides the editor.
  ⚠ **THE COMPUTE CEILING IS AN OVERRIDE ON THE DERIVED VALUE, NEVER A WRITE TO `RigState.totalCycles`.** `ComputeLadder.reconcile` recomputes that field from the items held on every load — which is precisely the anti-cheat property derivation exists for — so a cheat that assigned it would be reverted silently and read as a slider that does not work. `ComputeLadder.capacityOf` consults the override instead, keeping one answer to "what is this rig's ceiling". Negative-tested.
  ⚠ **THE INTRUSION SCALE MOVES THE CHANCE AND NEVER THE DRAW.** Both roll sites draw unconditionally so a replay from a stored seed stays a replay; scaling the draw would break that contract for every later roll in the session.
  ⚠ **A GRANT WRITES NO LEDGER ROW.** The ledger is the chain's record of value moving between addresses and this money did not move — a row would be a transaction with no counterparty. Every cheat writes to the **rig log at WARNING** instead, so a balance a player cannot account for a week later has an entry saying where it came from.
  ⚠ **A SOLVED BREACH STILL RESOLVES** — loot, noise, heat, foothold and the counter-hack roll all land. Skipping the resolution would leave `NetRules.reconcileFootholds` nothing to reconcile, so the target would read as unbreached and refuse a shell: a cheat whose visible effect is the machine not opening. Cleared, never bypassed, because `02` §2.4's proof-of-skill needs the class *solved*.
  ⚠ **THE REVEAL GRANTS DISCOVERY AND IDENTITY, NEVER A FOOTHOLD**, and it lives in `NetRules` rather than in the cheat class — what a discovery consists of is a network rule, and a second copy would be a machine that appeared on the map and then behaved unlike one a sweep found.
  ⚠ **USING a cheat pins the page visible (`CheatState.revealed`); entering the code does not.** Entering it is a look; applying one leaves state behind, and a character carrying a disabled thermal budget with no visible control to re-enable it is one that looks broken rather than cheated. `reset` deliberately does **not** clear the flag.
  ⚠ **A MODIFIER PRESS IS NOT A WRONG KEY**, which is the detail that makes the sequence enterable at all: holding Shift fires a `KEY_PRESSED` for `SHIFT` first, so a matcher that reset on anything unexpected would reset on the modifier of the very step it was waiting for — at step five, every time.
  ⚠ **`-Ddeck.cheats=1` on `DeckSnapshot`**, because a synchronous render delivers no key events and would otherwise photograph the one state indistinguishable from the feature being broken.
  ⚠ **TWO WAYS OUT, AND THE SECOND RESETS AS WELL AS HIDING.** *Reset to defaults* clears every override and leaves the page. *Turn developer options off* (`Cheats.conceal`) clears them **and** takes the page off the sidebar — one act, because hiding while leaving an override in force creates exactly the state `revealed` was added to prevent. `settingsBody`'s rebuild had to learn that its selected category can be **removed**, or the detail pane goes blank beside a working sidebar.
  ⚠ **"GAIN ALL INFO" FILLS EVERY RECON FILE ON THE MAP**, through `NodeReports.learnEverything` → the same `PortScanRules.findings` + `merge` a real settle uses, so a filled file is shaped exactly like a scanned one. ⚠ **Discovered machines only**, so it composes with the reveal instead of quietly doing its job too — a file on a machine the map has never heard of would show in RECON as a report about something absent from the map. ⚠ **Not counted as a scan** (the counters are restored around the merge): a file reporting scans nobody ran puts a detection ratio beside it that is a fraction of a number that never happened, which is why `establishIdentity` declines to bump it either.
  ⚠ **A BRIDGE CAN NEVER REACH `known() == 1.0`, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THE BUTTON'S COUNT DID NOT KNOW THAT.** Its ladder includes `PEERS` and `MONITORED`, which the recon file has nowhere to store (`17` §8 **PS-4**), so "is there anything left to learn?" asked as `known() < 1.0` answers *yes, forever* — **measured at 14 bridges** on a revealed map, i.e. a control permanently enabled reporting work it could never finish. `NodeReports.fullyLearned` is the answerable form and is what both the fill and the count use. Caught by the test, not by review.

  ⚠ **NOTHING ANYWHERE ELSE IN THE GAME REFERS TO IT.** No window in `WindowSpec`, no shell command, no man page, no term file, no i18n bundle entry, and it is deliberately absent from the Notices page's facility list. The sidebar category exists only once revealed. ⚠ **The one trace is the `cheat` facility in the rig log, and it is written only by a cheat that changed something** — which is the audit trail a player needs a week later when their balance does not add up, and it cannot appear on a character that never used one. ⚠ **Concealing therefore logs nothing at all**, in the rig log or the client log: it is the single action here available to a character that was never altered, and a line naming the facility written by the act of tidying it away would be the one place the game admits the feature exists to somebody who has not found it. `CheatsTest.concealLeavesNoTrace`, negative-tested against an unconditionally-logging `reset`.

- 2026-08-09 — **A breach that resolves as it OPENS never took the machine** — `GameEngine.beginBreach`, `FootholdAfterBreachTest.aBreachResolvedAtBeginReachesTheMap`. Reported as "auto breach does not count the breach as solved". The developer facility's *open every breach pre-solved* resolves inside `BreachRules.begin`, which makes **begin** the call that clears the last layer — but only `breachAction` and `resume` settled outcomes, because until auto-clear existed a breach could not possibly be finished by the act of opening one. So the attempt reported success, a `BREACHED` resolution was filed, `activeBreach.outcome` read `BREACHED` — and `reconcileFootholds` never ran, so the target stayed `contact` on the map and refused a shell. ⚠ **The third time the JOIN has been the defect rather than either side of it** (`reconcileFootholds` having no caller at all; `DeskManager.Spec.onClosed` being dropped). ⚠ **Fixed in `beginBreach`, not in the cheat**: the obligation is "whoever can finish a breach settles it", and putting it on the one caller that happens to finish one today leaves the next one to rediscover this. Free on the ordinary path — idempotent by construction, and a freshly opened breach has no resolution to reconcile. ⚠ Verified against the unfixed code, where the first two assertions pass and only the foothold fails — which is what located it.

- 2026-08-09 — **Every paragraph on every Settings page ellipsised instead of wrapping** — `Views.settingsPage`. Pre-existing since the sidebar layout landed, recorded in CLAUDE.md as needing its own pass, and found here by rendering the new Developer page. `settingsBody` sets `detail.setFitToHeight(true)` so a short category fills the pane; its comment claimed that was "ignored, correctly, whenever a category is taller than the window" and **it is not** — `fitToHeight` shrinks content to the viewport as far as the content's own *minimum* allows, a `VBox`'s minimum is the sum of its children's, and a `wrapText` Label's minimum is **one line**. So every tall category was squeezed, every three-line note was given one, and a squeezed `wrapText` Label **ellipsises rather than scrolling** — `...` mid-sentence, on exactly the notes that exist to say what a setting costs before somebody changes it. ⚠ Fixed with `page.setMinHeight(USE_PREF_SIZE)`, so `fitToHeight` can still grow a short page and can no longer shrink a tall one — which is what the original comment believed was already true. ⚠ `SecurityCenterView` records the identical trap one window along; this is the same one, panel-wide. ⚠ **Verified by rendering** a tall page and a short one, on the deck and on uOS Classic: no assertion could have caught it, and it had survived every green build since it shipped.

- 2026-08-08 — **The breach is a standalone window again — UI-8 resolved** — on explicit direction. `NetworkView` (BREACH tab removed), `EyeAndSickleClient` (`BREACH_WINDOW` via `showShell`), `BreachArming` (`focusBreach` deleted), `DeckShell.saveLayout` (the network window restores again). The reasoning is under **UI-8** in §2, which this closes rather than amends.

- 2026-08-08 — **Servers have generated names, and the network map is one tab per server** — on explicit direction, from the same sketch. [`18-network-topology.md`](18-network-topology.md) §2.5–2.6. `NpcNames.{CHARACTERS,server,looksLikeServer}`, `ServerTabs`, `NetLayout`, `NetMapView`; `ServerTabsTest`, `NpcNamesTest.Servers`. ⚠ **`adjective-character`**, 878 names across the fifteen franchises named in the request, hashed from the server id and de-collided by walking — no RNG draws, the same rule machine names follow. ⚠ **The pool that replaced it was seven names shared by every world in existence**, with its own note conceding it because "nobody replays a world for its place names" — true until a server became a **tab** the player picks between and a thing a bridge advertises by name. ⚠ **Fictional where `PIONEERS` is real, so the binding rule differs**: no real person (`zidane` reads as the footballer), no species (`wicked-necron` names a race), no ordinary given name (`wicked-sam` is not a Death Stranding reference), and no collision with the other three pools — Resident Evil's Karl **Heisenberg** was dropped because `PIONEERS` has Werner and a name in both reads as the physicist. ⚠ **Layers rebase on the shallowest machine in the filtered map**, a no-op for the whole-world map, so a foreign server's tab does not open with five empty columns. ⚠ **An unexplored tab is dimmed, never hidden**: a bridge naming its far side is the entire product of the bridge finding (`07` §5.1a).

- 2026-08-08 — **A server has a chosen shape: node depth 4–13, branch 1–7, at least two forks, and flat difficulty** — on explicit direction, from a sketch. [`18-network-topology.md`](18-network-topology.md) is the design, solo and online; the solo half is built. `Balance.{NET_NODE_DEPTH_*,NET_BRANCH_*,NET_MIN_BRANCHING_NODES,NET_SPINE_BUDGET_SHARE,netNodeDepth,netBranchWidth,netTier}`, `TopologyGenerator.buildServerTree`, `ServerShapeTest`. ⚠ **The shape was an ACCIDENT before this**: every machine attached to a uniformly chosen predecessor, so depth was about `log(count)` and the branch factor was whatever fell out — `../client/09` §8 had already measured it and filed it ("fan-out does not occur at reachable depth"), and the map's stack fold was built for a fan the generator could never produce. ⚠ **Zero new draws.** The spine costs the same `n − 1` values the random tree did, and the depth is paid for by **the reserved padding draw** whose own note has said since it was written that it exists "so a future per-server property can be added without shifting every downstream host's stream" — `nextInt(1)` and `nextDouble()` both step the stream once. ⚠ **Chords had to become same-layer**, or a single one collapses the spine after it is built — the server-level chord rule one level down, and it had nothing to apply to until a spine existed. ⚠ **Difficulty narrowed to 55/40/5 within a depth row** (§4.1), measured over ordinary machines because infrastructure keeps its +1. ⚠ Three negative tests run: the old table, the old tree and unconstrained chords each fire a different assertion.

- 2026-08-09 — **A machine that has more to give says so: the `5?` link estimate on the map** — on explicit direction, from a mockup. [`07-recon-tools.md`](07-recon-tools.md) §5.1b. `Sighting.linkEstimate`, `Balance.{netLinkEstimate,NET_LINK_ESTIMATE_ACCURACY_PERCENT}`, `NetRules.linkEstimate`, `NetCanvas.linkTag`, `NetGraph.describe`; `LinkEstimateTest`, `NetTypesTest`.

  **The gap it closes.** §5.1 sells WIDE and DEEP as *sensitivity, not reach* — and that is invisible from the player's side, because a re-sweep that finds nothing looks exactly like a re-sweep that could never have found anything. The two ethecoin upgrades were a purchase made blind. A machine now carries a rough count of its connections while any are unfound, so the question the ladder exists to pose — *is another sweep from here worth its cycles?* — has an answer on screen, without the map ever saying what would be found.

  ⚠ **THE ABSENCE IS THE INFORMATION, not the number**, and the suppression is therefore computed in the rules from the truth. A renderer comparing the estimate against the edges it had drawn would get it wrong in **both** directions, because the estimate is a band: it would hold the tag up forever on some machines and drop it early on others.

  ⚠ **IT IS THE ONLY FIELD ON `Sighting` THAT IS A CLAIM ABOUT UNDISCOVERED MACHINES**, so it clears that record's bar on a different argument from every other field and the argument is written into `NetTypesTest` rather than waved through: it is the **instrument's own sensitivity**, which is the licence `SweepReport.inRange` already stands on, and it carries no address, no type, no tier and no value. Four properties keep it there — suppressed when nothing is missing, wrong by up to 30%, **hashed rather than drawn** so re-sweeping cannot triangulate it, and cross-server links excluded so it cannot answer `18` §2.7a's question for free. What would fail the bar is the same figure exact, or without the suppression: either is the completion metric `18` §2.7c refuses to publish.

  ⚠ **Floored above what is already drawn.** A symmetric band on a five-link machine with four found can land on three, and "about 3" beside four visible lines reads as a broken instrument rather than as an estimate.

  ⚠ **TWO ACCURACY CONSTANTS THAT LOOK MERGEABLE AND ARE NOT.** 70 here against the bridge survey's 60, because they band different questions at different scales: a server's population runs to twenty-odd, a link count runs 1–7, and ±40% of 2 is noise wearing a number.

  ⚠ **NOT DRAWN WHERE THE SKETCH PUT IT, and the reason is a contrast rule rather than laziness.** The mockup hangs the tag in the corridor beside the machine; the corridor is the map's *gap* label at `-es-rule-hi`, one of the three tokens `ContrastTest` exempts from its 3:1 floor **because they draw hairlines**, so a number a player must read would repeat the defect this map shipped once with CONTACT and LOCKED at 1.77:1. It rides the cell's interior line in four columns that line has always padded — width-neutral by construction, so it cannot shear the column. Reaching the sketch's placement needs the tag to become its own node overlaid on the gap; recorded as open in §5.1b.

  ⚠ **Verified by rendering, and the first render looked like a failure and was not.** At `-Ddeck.reposition=3` no machine carried a tag — correct, because a walked graph has found its neighbourhood. At reposition 0 the same harness shows `TERM [#] 5?`, `STOR [#] 2?` beside untagged GATE and BRID boxes, on both the dark deck and uOS Classic, every box still 18 columns wide. ⚠ **A bridge correctly shows nothing** even with its far side unfound, which is property 3 visible in a picture.

- 2026-08-09 — **The data cache is gone: a successful breach mints no item at all** — on explicit direction. `BreachRules.resolveOffensive`, `GameEngine.purgeDataCaches`; `BreachRulesTest.offensiveLootIsNeverMoney` (rewritten), `SaveBackfillTest.dataCachesAreCleared`.

  ⚠ **IT WAS COMPLETELY INERT AND ONLY EVER CONSUMED A SLOT.** `"data-cache"` appeared in exactly one place in the codebase — the line that created it. It is not in `Catalogue`, so `Repac.sellable` answers false and `resaleValue` is zero; nothing anywhere reads its type; and storage offers a move between tiers and **no discard**, so there was no in-game action that removed one. Every successful offensive breach added one, forever. Reported from a real save holding **19 of 23 items** as data caches, filling 19 of 20 standard-storage slots.

  ⚠ **REMOVING IT DOES NOT LEAVE A BREACH YIELDING NOTHING, and that was the check that made it safe.** The reward for taking a machine is the **foothold** — reach, which `07` §5.1a makes the one thing no purchase buys — plus the one-time `host.lootWei` payout `NetRules.reconcileFootholds` credits from a finite stock placed at generation. Both land a moment later via `GameEngine.settleBreachOutcomes`. The item was a placeholder standing in for a reward that already existed elsewhere, and it was covering for the fact that the outcome slate never named the foothold; it does now.

  ⚠ **The I1/I2 test was rewritten rather than deleted, and it got stronger.** It asserted "an offensive breach yields an item, never ethecoin" — the half that carries the invariant is that this engine mints **no currency**, and it now asserts that **nothing** is minted, plus that the success is still *stated* (`design/05` §1 constraint 4: a result that says nothing reads as "the game decided").

  ⚠ **Two stale comments corrected in the same pass.** `HostState.lootWei` and `NetRules.reconcileFootholds` both said "the breach engine still mints a data cache and no currency" as half of the argument for why an ethecoin payout is not a faucet. That half is void, so the stock-not-a-flow reasoning is now the *whole* argument — flagged in both, because a load-bearing premise that quietly became false is the failure mode this file exists to catch.

  ⚠ **`GameEngine.purgeDataCaches` is the SECOND sanctioned exception to the no-legacy-machinery rule**, after `TopologyGenerator.relabelLegacy`, and it qualifies on the same footing: what it removes has no mechanical consequence, so the removal cannot change an outcome. Without it a character carrying nineteen carries them for good — the only other remedy being "delete your character", which is exactly what justified `relabelLegacy`. **Keyed on the item type and nothing else** — not `origin == "breached"`, which real loot would share. Silent when it finds nothing; logs when it removes something, because a storage count that drops between sessions is owed a reason. **Delete it the moment a build ships.**

- 2026-08-09 — **Nothing in the shipped game could establish a machine's TYPE, so a DEEP sweep and a foothold now do — and the far side of a bridge got dearer in the same pass** — on explicit direction. `NetRules.{identify,settleSweep,reconcileFootholds,sighting,revealAll}`, `NodeState.kind`, `HostState.identified`; `IdentificationTest`, amended `CheatsTest.Reveal`.

  ⚠ **THE GAP IS THAT THE RULE HAD NO IMPLEMENTATION, AND SIX DOC COMMENTS DEFENDED IT ANYWAY.** `NodeState.kind` was assigned in exactly one place in the whole codebase — to `"UNKNOWN"`, in `NetRules.nodeFor` — and `HostState.identified` only by `TopologyGenerator` (the player's own rig) and `revealAll` (the developer cheat). The 15 EC **Passive Sniffer** that nine comments in `engine/net` defer to **is not in `Catalogue`**, and no `PortScanTarget` rung sells a type either. So no action available to a player could type a machine: **every box on the network map read `----` forever**, and the map's whole type vocabulary — `NetGlyphs.NODE_BRIDGE`, the woven bridge frame, the `BRIDGE_RAISED`/`BRIDGE_LOWERED` drawbridge, the `··` stub, `es-netmap-bridge`, `es-netmap-identified`, `Targets.role` — was unreachable outside the cheat. Measured on a real 575-host save: **one** identified host (the rig), all nineteen discovered nodes `UNKNOWN`.

  ⚠ **THE ONE TEST THAT LOOKED ASSERTED THE BUG.** `NetRulesTest.aSweepNeverNamesAType` walked every node and asserted `kind == UNKNOWN` — true, and true for a reason nobody had checked: it was true of *every* action, not of sweeps. This is `design/02` §5's pricing check appearing to pass because the priced tool does not exist. **A test that asserts an absence passes just as well when the presence is unreachable.**

  ⚠ **The reported symptom was about bridges and named the wrong system**, the second time in two days (see the crossing entry below). "Two machines that should have been bridges are drawn as ordinary boxes" was true, and bridge-specific only by coincidence — nothing was typed, and bridges are simply where it shows, because they are the one kind whose type changes what the player can *do*.

  **The rule, as directed.** A **DEEP** sweep types what it picks up; a **foothold** types the machine, on `NodeReports.establishIdentity`'s reasoning — standing on a machine, its name, its account and its kind are the three facts you cannot avoid. **BASE and WIDE still sell existence and adjacency and nothing else**, which is what leaves the Passive Sniffer a real product the day it ships: typing a machine found cheaply and not broken into.

  ⚠ **AND THE FAR SIDE OF A BRIDGE GOT STRICTER IN THE SAME EDIT, because typing got cheaper.** `Sighting.bridgePeerServerName` was published off `kind == BRIDGE` alone. That was already the wrong rule by [`18-network-topology.md`](18-network-topology.md) §2.8 — which reversed "breaching is enough to list the far server" within a day — and it became live the instant a foothold started typing: without the narrowing, breaking into a bridge would name the network behind it for free. It is now `surveyed || netMan`, **the same condition that puts the tab on the strip**, deliberately, so the name of the far side and a place to go to it arrive together instead of from two rules that can disagree.

  ⚠ **It writes `NodeState.kind` and NOT `HostState.identified`**, and `revealAll` was moved onto the same door. "The player has established what this is" is the player's knowledge, so it belongs on the `knownNodes` row that the map **and** `Targets.role` both read — the old flag reached the map only, so a revealed machine drew as TERM there and blank in the breach window. `HostState.identified` is now vestigial (only the rig writes it, and `sighting` already answers that from `self`); kept rather than deleted because it is a persisted field.

  ⚠ **Two `CheatsTest` assertions moved from the flag to the row.** The property they protect — a reveal must grant every machine what it grants any machine — is untouched, and asserting the row is the stronger claim, because that is what a screen renders from.

  ⚠ **The honest consequence, stated rather than hidden:** a machine discovered by a BASE or WIDE sweep is never typed by a DEEP sweep *afterwards*, because `beginSweep` builds its `found` list only from machines not yet discovered, so no later sweep will ever hand it back. Breaching it is the other route, and re-sweeping is deliberately not a reroll.

  ⚠ **`NodeReports.known` is untouched**, so typing a machine does not shift which puzzle a breach draws (`Balance.breachProtocolShare`). A type is not a port-scan rung.

  ⚠ **Verified by rendering, not by the suite.** `DeckSnapshot -Ddeck.netdump=1` now prints `GATE`/`TERM`/`RELA`/`STOR` where every box read `----`, and a swept-but-unbreached bridge draws `╪╪ BRID [#]` in its woven frame with no far-side name; the PNG shows a breached one as `|/\|BRID [/]`, crossing shut. ⚠ **Found on the way: `-Ddeck.netdump=1` prints BEFORE `-Ddeck.reveal=1` is applied** (`sweepTheNeighbourhood` is called at line 308, the reveal runs at 407), so the text dump can never show a revealed map. Pre-existing, not fixed here.

- 2026-08-09 — **A breach target's refusal was enforced by the CLIENT and by nothing else** — `BreachRules.begin`, `BreachRulesTest.Opening`. `Targets` computes a refusal per target — a shut crossing, a machine already breached, a rig that cannot afford the attempt — and sets `available` from it; `Targets.byId` handed an un-attemptable target back unchanged and `begin` never looked. Only `BreachTargetList` and `BreachCommands` checked, and **the map's route asks neither**: BREACH arms `node:<address>` unconditionally and START BREACH hands the armed id straight to `beginBreach`. ⚠ **Observed on a real save, twelve seconds apart in one rig log**: a DEEP survey printed *"Nothing over there answers until a NET_MAN is running on this bridge"*, and the machine at the far end was then breached, looted for 16.1 EC and left holding a foothold on a server `NetRules.crossable` says is unreachable. ⚠ **Refused BEFORE the compute reservation**, because `Targets` says the order is the message: a machine behind a shut crossing is not an attempt the player is one purchase of cycles away from. The compute check below it survives and is still the authoritative one — `reserve` actually attempts, where `available` is a snapshot. ⚠ This is the client-authoritative arrangement **I14** exists to forbid, arriving through a field nobody had wired.

- 2026-08-09 — **Crossings: a sweep never reaches onto another server, a well-mapped server gives up its exits, and a NET_MAN opens the door** — on explicit direction. [`18-network-topology.md`](18-network-topology.md) §2.7a–2.7c and §2.8 are the design; `Balance.{NET_BRIDGE_REVEAL_SHARE,NETMAN_PRICE_EC,NETMAP_UPLOAD_*,NET_PEER_ESTIMATE_ACCURACY_PERCENT,netPeerEstimate}`, `HostState.{surveyed,netMan}`, `NodeState.{peerEstimate,peerAccuracyPercent}`, `NetRules.{serverCompletion,crossable,openCrossing,uploadNetMan,completeNetMan}`, `Catalogue.NETMAN_ID`, `NetGlyphs.BRIDGE_{RAISED,LOWERED}`; `CrossingTest`.
  ⚠ **The report that prompted it was "my home server generated no bridge", and that was a misdiagnosis worth recording**: every world has one, pinned at **400/400**. What was true is that a first WIDE/DEEP sweep from home found it in only **75%** of worlds and re-sweeping is not a reroll — a discovery gap, not a generation one. **Measure before designing a fix; the reported symptom named the wrong system.**
  ⚠ **A sweep reaching onto another server was a REAL LEAK and nothing had ever caught it** — hop distance is a BFS over the full link graph, so a two-hop ceiling from a bridge put the far bridge's neighbours in range. It rarely produced a *discovery*, which is why the regression test asserts on `inRange` (what was considered) rather than on what was found: the first version of that test passed with the rule deleted.
  ⚠ **The completion metric is hidden by decision.** Publishing it would be a count of undiscovered machines wearing a percentage, which is the one thing the discovery model forbids.
  ⚠ **I2 was read deliberately rather than assumed.** NET_MAN is ethecoin-gated and is the only route to every server but home; what makes that breadth rather than a ceiling is that it is consumed **once per crossing** and the crossing stays open, so the lifetime cost is bounded by the world's size. ⚠ Its **price is therefore load-bearing** in a way an ordinary consumable's is not — re-tuning mining income downward without moving it closes the world.
  ⚠ **§2.8 reversed within the day of being written.** "Breaching is enough to list the far server" was true only while a sweep could reach across; once it could not, a breach-only tab was a named, permanently empty server. Both the doc and its test asserted the old rule and both were rewritten.
- 2026-08-08 — **A sweep's detection is a property of the (machine, vantage) PAIR, and the yield band is 1–11** — on explicit direction. [`07-recon-tools.md`](07-recon-tools.md) §5.1a is the design; `Balance.{NET_SWEEP_VANTAGE_FLOOR,netSweepAudibility,sweepYield}`, `NetRules.audibility`, `VantageDiscoveryTest`. The ask was that repositioning reveal machines and bridges a sweep from the rig could not, building a large graph over time. ⚠ **The rule it replaces was the reason that did not already happen**: `detectRoll` was the machine's alone, so a contact missed from one position was missed from *every* position at that tier and moving bought reach and only reach. ⚠ **It is a HASH, not a draw**, which is the single line that keeps `SweepDeterminismTest.resweepingIsNotAReroll` true — "chance of discovery" reads exactly like a per-sweep roll and implemented as one it would have made repetition the cheapest strategy in the game. ⚠ **A multiply, not an additive spread**, so the home floor's forced `detectRoll = 0` stays zero from everywhere. ⚠ Measured over 300 worlds and 12 positions: staying home 5.1 machines, walking 10.1 at base and 19.8 at wide. ⚠ **Still open**: `NET_SWEEP_BRIDGE_MIN_TIER` is untouched, so a base-only player still cannot see a bridge from anywhere and their graph plateaus on the home server — see §2 **NET-1**.

- 2026-08-08 — **The top strip's heat readout is labelled by a drawn eye instead of the words `PERSONAL HEAT`** — on explicit direction. `client/ui/widgets/EyeMark` (+ `EyeMarkTest`), `DeckShell`, `UiTokens.HEAT_MARK_*`, `theme.css`, `DeckSnapshot`. The cell keeps its anatomy — something over the meter, as every other strip cell is a key over a value — and only the key changes from a word to a mark. The reasoning, what it spends against `../client/01` §2.2.4 and `../client/07` §5.2, and the test that keeps `ui-design-language.md` §9's icon-set ban intact are recorded under **UI-8** in §2, which this amends rather than replaces.

- 2026-08-08 — **The port scanner offered the two bridge rungs on every machine in the game** — `client/view/PortScanView`, `PortScanViewTest`. `PortScanTarget.appliesTo` has said since 2026-08-07 that `PEERS` and `MONITORED` exist on a **bridge** and nowhere else, and the engine honoured it on both sides while the panel walked `values()` blind: an ordinary desktop listed *Peers* and *Monitoring* with a calibrated price, duration and detection risk against an answer that is `-1` on anything but a bridge. ⚠ **The filter reads `Sighting.kind` — what the player has established — never the topology's own kind**, or the two rows would appear on unidentified bridges *and nowhere else*, which hands the Passive Sniffer's whole product (`07` §1) to anyone who right-clicks a machine. An unidentified bridge shows the ordinary eight. ⚠ The findings block below walks the **same list**, which closed a live gap in the other direction — the two bridge findings had been reachable and unrenderable since they landed. ⚠ What is still unbuilt is their **storage**: see `17-bridges-and-surveillance.md` §8 **PS-4**.

- 2026-08-08 — **The map's fold was measured dormant, so it now folds whole BRANCHES — and the player can fold one by hand** — [`../client/09-network-map-graph.md`](../client/09-network-map-graph.md) §3.3–3.5. `NetLayout`, `UiTokens`, `NetGraph`, `NetCanvas`, `NetMapView`, `GameSession`, `engine/rules/MapFolds`, `GameSave.netFolds`; `NetLayoutTest`, `MapFoldsTest`, `FoldCensus`.

  ⚠ **THE FEATURE WAS BUILT, CORRECT AND ALMOST NEVER FIRING, and only a census showed it.** Draw, click, keyboard, expand, collapse-back and screen-reader text all shipped on 2026-08-08 and were all reachable; `NetLayoutTest` was green throughout. Measured over **twelve generated worlds walked eight repositions each**: **one stack**. Three gates, each on its own sufficient — `NET_STACK_MIN_LAYER` blocked 12 of the 13 wide parents (they are all at layer 1), the peel then reduced those 12 to **zero** eligible members, and past layer 1 branch widths run 1–4 against a threshold of 4. **A green suite cannot see a feature that never runs**; `FoldCensus` is the tool, and it is kept for that reason.
  ⚠ **LOWERING `NET_STACK_MIN_LAYER` WOULD HAVE CHANGED NOTHING**, which was the obvious first move. The peel had already reduced those parents to zero — the binding constraint was one layer down from where it looked.
  ⚠ **A MEMBER MAY NOW HAVE A CHILD, BECAUSE THE CHILD COMES IN.** `09` §3.2 requires the collapsed edge to be one honest edge, and excluding a machine with children satisfies it — but the generator builds every server around a spine (`18` §2), so almost every machine has one and the rule excluded almost everything. Folding the child **too** satisfies the same requirement, and it is what makes a fold answer the pressure the map actually has. The disqualifying case is now the only one: a member with a neighbour outside the branch. ⚠ The peel is still a **fixpoint** and the cascade is longer now — one bad lateral deep in a spine peels every machine above it back to the fork, which is the right answer and not a near miss.
  ⚠ **WHAT FOLDS ON ITS OWN IS A FORK, NEVER A CHAIN — `NET_STACK_MIN_FORK`.** A branch counts everything behind it, so a size-only rule collapses a corridor on sight and a fresh map opens reading `rig → a → ×14`, hiding the one structure the player is walking. Depth is folded **by the player**, who is the only party that knows which way they are working. Measured after: **11 of 12 worlds fold something on their own**, 6–17 machines each; maps go from 3–7 columns to mostly 3.
  ⚠ **`NET_STACK_MIN_LAYER` NOW BOUNDS THE CANDIDATES, not only the automatic ones.** Once a branch can be folded by hand, a candidate at layer 1 is **one menu click that folds the entire discovered world** into a box hanging off `SELF` — the `rig → ×7` defect reached deliberately rather than by accident. The rig offers no branch; every real machine does.
  ⚠ **TRAILING COLUMNS ARE TRIMMED, and that is new.** A same-layer fold never emptied a column; a branch fold absorbs everything behind it. Left in, the map keeps a header and a blank field for a hop range holding nothing — which reads as *"there is something out there you cannot see"*, the one thing this surface may never say. Headers are built from what is **drawn** for the same reason.
  ⚠ **`Result.stacks()` AND `Result.branches()` ARE TWO LISTS AND CONFLATING THEM IS WRONG BOTH WAYS.** `stacks` is what is drawn — what the renderer paints and `foldedMachines` counts. `branches` is what *could* be, open ones included, which is what a menu offering "collapse this" must consult. One list means either the renderer paints boxes nobody folded, or the menu can only offer to fold what is already folded.
  ⚠ **NM-1 IS REVERSED WITHIN THE DAY: folding persists, per character, in the save.** The original resolution was right about *expansion* and wrong about *folding*, and the difference is who decided — opening a box the map folded for you is undoing an automatic decision, folding a branch yourself is an arrangement worth keeping. ⚠ **On the port for I14's reason and not because it is a rule**: a fold names an address, and *"have I discovered this machine"* is a rules question the client may not answer, so `MapFolds.set` refuses an undiscovered one — which also bounds the map by the world rather than by clicks. ⚠ **Nothing in the rules reads it back**, pinned by `MapFoldsTest.foldsAreInert`. ⚠ **Open is stored and is not absent** — absent means the threshold decides, `false` means the player opened a branch that folds on its own, and without storing it the fold returns on every launch. ⚠ **A stale key survives**: pruning would delete a preference on a discovery.
  ⚠ **THE PRICE IS §3.4's INSERTION GUARANTEE, and it is not recoverable.** Expansion used to insert rows at the stack's own row so nothing above it moved; a branch fold's members belong to columns arranged without them, so there is no row to insert them at. What holds instead is that **a machine never changes column when a fold opens**, which is what the player navigates by. Weaker, stated, and tested.
  ⚠ **`RemoteGameSession` answers an EMPTY map and refuses a write**, as it does for folders — so in federated play the map falls back to its own threshold rather than claiming every branch is open. The transport is CL-8's, unbuilt.
  ⚠ **Discoverability was the last gap and it is a real one.** An unfolded map has no box to click and a right-click menu nobody opens is a menu nobody knows about, so the note line under the graph says *"Right-click a machine to fold what is behind it"* whenever there is a branch to fold, and a machine's spoken text names how many sit behind it. New as **NM-6** in `09` §8: there is still no index of what a player has folded.

- 2026-08-08 — **The network map's graph is rebuilt: the clamp is gone, forward edges reach their targets, and a wide fan folds into a stack** — [`../client/09-network-map-graph.md`](../client/09-network-map-graph.md), all five steps of its §7. `NetLayout`, `NetCanvas`, `NetGraph`, `NetGlyphs`, `UiTokens`, `theme.css`, `DeckSnapshot`.

  ⚠ **NOTHING IS HIDDEN ANY MORE. `NET_MAX_ROWS` AND `+N MORE` ARE DELETED.** A layer wider than sixty rows used to draw the first sixty and put the remainder in a header count — machines the player had found were in the map's data and absent from its picture, which is the one thing a map may not do. What replaces it is a **stack**: a fold of machines *already discovered*, always marked, always counted exactly, always openable. `NetLayoutTest.nothingIsEverDropped` asserts the replacement rule directly — over every fixture, drawn + folded == sightings.
  ⚠ **A STACK COUNTS ONLY MACHINES THE PLAYER HAS FOUND**, and that is the invariant the whole feature is written against. `NetRules` forbids publishing a count of undiscovered hosts — "*no placeholder, no count, no 'three contacts nearby'*" — so `×7` means seven discovered machines are folded behind this one and may never mean "seven links, of which you found two". The bridge peer count stays the sanctioned exception, in the port-scan report.
  ⚠ **THE TEN-COLUMN SPACE IS FIXED, AND SO IS ITS MIRROR IMAGE NOBODY HAD REPORTED.** Every forward arrow on the map pointed into blank space, because the run stopped at the end of the 3-column gap while the next box was ten columns further on. Lateral edges had the same defect from the other side: their bracket sat at the *start* of the strip and stopped **eight columns short** of the machine it joined. Both are fixed by moving the bracket against the box (`NET_LATERAL_BUS_COLS`) and running forward edges the whole 13-column corridor.
  ⚠ **"ROUTE AROUND THOSE TWO COLUMNS" COMES TO ONE CELL DECLINED**, because in a grid everything going left to right crosses every column. A forward run **yields** at the lateral channel when it already carries ink, so the arc survives intact — merging would give `┴`, which is honest and still a loss, since the arc is the only thing telling a same-layer edge from a hop in greyscale. ⚠ **Laterals are drawn first and the order is load-bearing**: reversed, the forward run claims the empty cell and the arc is refused instead.
  ⚠ **`NET_STACK_MIN_LAYER` = 2 IS A BOUND THE DESIGN DID NOT HAVE, and it was found by rendering.** With a one-hop ceiling every machine a fresh sweep finds hangs off the rig, so eligibility alone folded the player's *entire* neighbourhood into one box and the headline surface read `rig → ×7`. Layer 1 is what the panel is for.
  ⚠ **A MEMBER MAY HAVE NO EDGE THAT LEAVES THE GROUP** — no second parent, no lateral outside it, no child of its own — or the collapsed edge stops being a single honest edge. Two rules enforce it and each masked the other in negative testing, so the property that holds the line is asserted in general form (`noAdjacencyIsLost`).
  ⚠ **THE ARRANGEMENT IS TWO PASSES AND EXPANSION CANNOT REACH IT.** Forward then backward, never "until stable"; both run over **collapsed** units whatever is open, which is what makes opening a fold insert rows without re-sorting the layer. Ties still break on address.
  ⚠ **`DeckSnapshot` NOW SWEEPS — the prerequisite for all of the above.** It ran no sweep, so no render this project could produce contained a single edge. It grants the Topology Mapper and a deep sweep, commissions a real sweep, and settles it by winding an advanceable `Clock`; `-Ddeck.reposition=N` walks the traversal loop and `-Ddeck.netdump=1` prints the grid as text.
  ⚠ **TWO PRE-EXISTING HARNESS DEFECTS SURFACED ON THE FIRST REAL RENDER.** It built its engine with `GameEngine.open` rather than `TestSaves.bare`, so the rig was a **24-cycle** starting rig and `allocateSelfMining(30)` and `scan("thorough")` (35) were both silently refused — the deck photographed with an idle compute grid and a SECURITY CENTER reading *"Unaudited"*. Written against a 100-cycle rig; nothing re-checked it when the compute ladder landed on 2026-08-06.
  ⚠ **AND THE MEASUREMENT INVERTS THE DESIGN'S OWN PRIORITY.** Over seven generated worlds, layers run **1–5 machines wide** and maps run **4–10 columns deep**. Fan-out — the pressure `09` §2 lists first and the pressure stacks relieve — **does not occur at reachable depth**, because the hop ceiling is 2 and a sweep sees only a machine's surroundings. Depth is what grows, past any window. Stacks are built, correct and currently **dormant**; `NET_STACK_THRESHOLD` is **left at 4 rather than tuned down on three samples**. New as **NM-5** in `09` §8: the map's real unreadability is horizontal and nothing here addresses it.
  NM-1 and NM-3 resolved; NM-2 answered with data and left open; NM-4 unchanged.
  ⚠ **SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY by the entry above** — the dormancy was confirmed at twelve worlds, the fold was rebuilt around branches rather than fans, and **NM-1 and NM-2 both moved**. Do not reason from this paragraph: a stale "built but dormant" is exactly the load-bearing premise CLAUDE.md warns about.

- 2026-08-07 — **The network map's graph has a design: stacked nodes, expansion, and arrangement** — [`../client/09-network-map-graph.md`](../client/09-network-map-graph.md). **Nothing in it is built**; §1 records the renderer as it stands and everything else is `[PROPOSAL]`.

  ⚠ **THE CONSTRAINT THE WHOLE DESIGN IS WRITTEN AGAINST: a stack counts only machines the player has FOUND.** `NetRules` forbids publishing a count of undiscovered hosts — "*no placeholder, no count, no 'three contacts nearby'*" — so a stack is a folding of what is already on the map and never a hint about what is not. A stack reading 7 means seven discovered machines are collapsed behind it; it may never mean "this node has seven links, of which you found two", which would put a sweep's product on the graph for free. The bridge peer count stays the sanctioned exception, in the port-scan report where it was decided.
  ⚠ **GROUPED BY PARENT, and the alternatives were rejected for stated reasons.** By layer, the count answers nothing — it is "machines that happened to sort last". By kind or server, the collapsed edge would be a lie, and it hands out the Passive Sniffer's published function (`design/07` §1) for nothing.
  ⚠ **EXPANSION INSERTS ROWS; IT DOES NOT RE-SORT THE LAYER.** Re-running the barycentre pass with the members present would move machines the player was not looking at — the same defect as the vantage re-rooting the graph, one level down. The frame must not move when the player explores it.
  ⚠ **THE ARRANGEMENT STAYS A FIXED NUMBER OF PASSES.** One forward pass today, two proposed (forward then backward), never "until stable": the packet dot repaints on a timer and a layout that settled differently each time would make the whole graph shimmer. Ties still break on **address** — a tiebreak on tier or kind would leak a recon finding into the row order and reshuffle the map whenever a scan landed.
  ⚠ **TWO RENDERER DEFECTS ARE RECORDED AS PREREQUISITES, not folded in.** The ten-column space (forward arrows point into the next layer's lateral strip; the naive fix breaks `lateralEdgesUseArcs` and must route *around* the two columns lateral edges use), and — the bigger one — **`DeckSnapshot` has one host and runs no sweep, so no render this project can produce contains a single edge**. Every visual item below it is otherwise tuned blind, which is how two of three routing lanes came to render as stubs silently for as long as the token was wrong.
  ⚠ **Sequenced so each step is independently shippable**, with stacks-collapsed before expansion, and the layout heuristic **last** — once stacks exist, layer widths are bounded by the number of parents rather than the number of machines, so the arrangement's job is much smaller.
  Open as **NM-1…NM-4** in `09` §8.

- 2026-08-07 — **A base sweep can no longer find a BRIDGE, and bridges gained a surveillance layer in design** — `Balance.NET_SWEEP_BRIDGE_MIN_TIER`, `HostArchetypes.{detectableBySweep,acceptsDeployedWork}`, `NetRules.beginSweep`. New doc [`17-bridges-and-surveillance.md`](17-bridges-and-surveillance.md); **MonJob, the Tracer, distance-driven reprisal and the anti-trace minigame are `[PROPOSAL]` there and are not built.**

  ⚠ **THE SPEC AS GIVEN COLLIDED WITH I2 IN THREE PLACES, and was reshaped rather than implemented.** `SweepTier`'s charter is "ethecoin buys sensitivity, schematics buy reach": its tiers may add "no new hop, no new field, **no new class of node**", `NetRules.hopCeiling` deliberately **takes no sweep tier** ("there is no code path from ethecoin to reach — I2 is structural here, not a rule somebody remembers"), and `NetRules` forbids publishing a count of undiscovered machines ("**no placeholder, no count**, no 'three contacts nearby'"). Asked for as written — wide/deep sweeps discovering bridges, deep sweeps seeing *past* them, and a count of what is behind — all three would have been broken.
  ⚠ **WHAT SAVED IT IS THAT DISCOVERY AND REACH ARE DIFFERENT PURCHASES.** Bridges are already inside the hop ceiling and are already `SignalStrength.HIGH`; a base sweep found them **85% of the time**, so the way *out* of a server was the most reliable thing the free starting instrument could see. Withholding them below tier 2 is a **hard gate on candidacy** — which kinds a tier can hear — and leaves a player exactly as far from the next server as before. Crossing is still breach → foothold → `connect` → sweep again, still earned. `BridgeVisibilityTest.ReachIsUnchanged` asserts the ceiling does not move, and is the assertion to keep above all the others.
  ⚠ **GATEWAYS AND RELAYS ARE NOT GATED WITH BRIDGES**, though all three are the same signal strength. A gateway is a signpost — index 0, no loot, its function is to be the first thing found on a server — so hiding it would leave a base sweep finding nothing but quiet desktops on a server already reached. What is withheld is the way **onward**, and a test fires if the gate ever widens past that.
  ⚠ **A FLOOR, NEVER A BAND, because monotonicity is required.** `detected(T1) ⊆ detected(T2) ⊆ detected(T3)` is asserted from one vantage: a player who buys a better instrument must never lose a contact they already had.
  ⚠ **THE PEER COUNT IS A PORT-SCAN FINDING, NOT A SWEEP RESULT, and it is NOT YET BUILT because the insertion is a trap.** `PortScanTarget` is a strict 1–8 order and `PortScanRules` prices `depth − 1` *precisely because* adding `IDENTITY` at the bottom would otherwise have re-priced the seven above it. Worse: **`NodeReports.known` divides by `PortScanTarget.values().length`**, so a ninth bridge-only rung would cap **every ordinary machine in the game** at 8/9 — and `known` feeds `Balance.breachProtocolShare`, so breach-puzzle weighting would fall for every target a player ever scans, silently, with every screen still rendering. `known` has to divide by the rungs *applicable to that host* first. **PS-1/PS-2** in `17` §3.2.
  ⚠ **`HostArchetypes.acceptsDeployedWork` refuses bridges and NOTHING CALLS IT**, said out loud rather than hidden: there is no player deploy action in the engine — `NodeState.deployedMiners` is read in six places and written by none, because bots are `10` and deliberately unbuilt. A bridge is a router, not a computer, and **I6** is why it matters. ⚠ A rule with no caller is how `reconcileFootholds` stayed broken for weeks — **the deploy action must call this on the day it is written.**
  ⚠ **MonJob decisions taken the same day (MJ-1, MJ-2), both in `17` §4.** **Owner-removal**: the placer can remove their own and nobody else can — without it a player permanently pollutes every bridge they touch, including their own route home, which is a mess with no counterplay. ⚠ Removal is **silent to everyone else**, or a watched party learning a MonJob had been *taken off* is handed the one fact §4.1 withholds: that somebody was watching, and has stopped. **NPC density scales with depth from home** — rare near, common far — which is what stops the system being multiplayer-only content, since in solo the NPC ones are the only ones there are. ⚠ **Zero at home, declared as its own constant**, copying `NET_COUNTER_HACK_HOME`'s pattern *and* its stated reason ("a teaching space that occasionally plants a parasite on the student is a teaching space they learn to avoid"). ⚠ **Derived by hashing, never drawn** — one draw per bridge would re-roll every existing world, the trap `NpcNames` records as having bitten twice, and it must consume no RNG or `SweepDeterminismTest`'s draw-count assertions break.
  ⚠ **DENSITY ALONE TEACHES THE PLAYER NOTHING, and that is MJ-3.** A **tier-1 MonJob is invisible to the intruder by design**, so a player crossing ten watched bridges learns nothing and is then counter-hacked with no visible cause — `NetRules`' own words for that failure are *"a mechanic that punishes without explaining is indistinguishable from a bug."* Recommended: the **tier mix** shifts toward tier 2 with depth as well as the density, because tier 2 is the only thing that tells the player in words that the network noticed, and because distant NPCs are aggressive rather than cautious — tier 2's cost is being revealed, which an aggressive operator does not mind. It is also what gives the Tracer anything to read.
  ⚠ **THE ANTI-TRACE MINIGAME'S SCHEDULING OBJECTION IS WITHDRAWN — the breach puzzle is BUILT.** This entry first argued it should wait as "the game's second minigame while the first is unbuilt"; that came from a stale line in `CLAUDE.md` (now corrected) and was wrong on its facts. ⚠ **I10 survives the correction and is the harder half**: a timed defence is the shape automation is best at, and the breach's anti-bot property does not transfer — *"cross-referencing two documents is a simultaneity problem"* and a countdown is not one. `17` §7.2 gives the two ways out.

- 2026-08-07 — **The map's node menu breaches in ONE gesture, a narrow reversal of `BreachArming`'s two-step rule** — on explicit direction. Right-click a machine → **Breach** selects the BREACH tab and begins the attempt; `client/view/{BreachArming,NetworkView,BreachView}` and `EyeAndSickleClient.breach`.

  ⚠ **WHAT IS BEING REVERSED, AND FOR ONE ENTRY POINT ONLY.** `BreachArming` exists because *"the control that chooses a target and the control that commits to one must not be the same click"* — a breach reserves compute that aborting does not refund (`05` §4). That rule was written against a specific defect: the target **list** began an attempt the instant a row's button was pressed, **in a list that reflows whenever a sweep lands**, so you aimed at row three, a sweep landed, and row three was a different machine you had already committed to.
  ⚠ **THE EXEMPTION IS FENCED BY THAT HAZARD, NOT BY TASTE.** A context menu has none of it: opened *on* a node, anchored at the pointer, naming the act on its face, and nothing can reflow between choosing the machine and choosing the verb — they are **the same gesture on the same object**. ⚠ **The target list is untouched**; `BreachArmingTest.armingAloneNeverStarts` is the fence, and the map's own BREACH *chip* is also unchanged because it acts on a selection that may be stale.
  ⚠ **THE REQUEST IS KEYED ON THE TARGET ID, NEVER A BOOLEAN.** A bare flag is collected by whatever happens to be armed when it is next read — right-click Breach on a machine the rules refuse, nothing starts, the flag survives; then arm a different machine from the list and **that** one is committed to with no press at all. `takeStartRequest(id)` is test-and-clear in one call, answers only for the machine it was asked for, and **clears on a mismatch too**. Both guards negative-tested against the naive boolean version.
  ⚠ **IT RIDES ON THE ONE-PULSE SETTLE THAT ALREADY EXISTED.** `beginBreach` changes session state, which re-enters `refresh` through `onChange` — firing from `refresh` would begin a breach *during* the window's construction. The settle is also what makes it prompt: nothing re-runs `refresh` when the launch control goes live, and the session ticks about once a second.
  ⚠ **VERIFIED BY A PROBE THAT PUMPS THE FX QUEUE**, because neither a unit test nor a render can see this — the start is scheduled on `Platform.runLater`, and no queued runnable executes during a synchronous `Scene.snapshot`. Measured from a clean engine: menu path → tab `MAP → BREACH`, breach `false → true` (T1, 12 cycles reserved, live), armed cleared; list path → tab stays `MAP`, breach stays `false`, target still armed. ⚠ The probe's **first** version reported the fence broken and was wrong — it reused one run for both paths, and `dismissBreach` only clears a *resolved* attempt, so it measured the live breach from phase one.

- 2026-08-07 — **The core cutaway's rotation speed is the rig's load** — `client/ui/widgets/CoreCage`, driven from `RigStatus.load()`. 18s per revolution at the lightest load that turns, 3s with every cycle claimed; an idle rig still holds its frame.

  ⚠ **SPEED IS NOT THE PERIOD.** Varying the `Pulse` period is the obvious change and does not work: `Pulse` quantises every subscription to a multiple of its 100ms driver, silently, so the whole load range collapses onto 300/200/100 — three speeds — and every load change would tear down and re-establish the subscription. `SyncSpin` records the same trap from the other side. The tick is fixed at the driver's rate and the speed lives in **how far it turns per tick**.
  ⚠ **WHICH NEEDS A FRACTIONAL POSITION.** Whole steps per tick can only express 14.4s, 7.2s and 4.8s per revolution, and the fast end would jump 15° a frame. A double accumulator sits behind the integer step the render has always used.
  ⚠ **TICKING 3× MORE OFTEN IS NOT 3× THE WORK** — `advance` re-renders only when the integer step actually changes, so the redraw rate follows the rotation rather than the clock (2.7/s idle, 16/s at full load).
  ⚠ **THE RATE DOES NOT RAMP TO ZERO, DELIBERATELY.** A crawling rig would be indistinguishable from a stopped one, so "stopped" would stop meaning "idle" — the widget's clearest reading. Stopping is the guard's job (`load <= 0 && heat <= 0`), never the rate's.
  ⚠ **THE GUARD NOW ASKS ABOUT LOAD RATHER THAN EARNING**: a rig holding cycles on an armed defensive array and mining nothing used to sit still and read as idle, which was false. ⚠ **`load` is TAKEN, not derived** — `RigStatus.load()` is the one definition and the figure beside the panel reads off it, so a second copy computed in the widget could disagree with the readout, which is `ChainState.networkHashrate`'s trap.

- 2026-08-07 — **CL-7 closed: the client has an audio engine** — `client/sound/` and [`../client/08-audio.md`](../client/08-audio.md). One `SourceDataLine`, software mixing, polyphony with voice stealing, MUSIC and EFFECTS buses plus master, ramped ducking, streamed music with an equal-power crossfade, a closed effect catalogue with per-effect gain and retrigger guard, and seven machine-wide settings.

  ⚠ **THE ONE-`Clip`-PER-SOUND SHAPE FAILS ON THE SECOND SOUND, FOR THREE INDEPENDENT REASONS**, and only the first is the one people expect. A `Clip` has **one playback cursor**, so the same effect twice in a second restarts rather than layers — two messages arriving together make one noise, and fixing it needs a *pool per effect*. Every open `Clip` **holds a mixer line**; measured here every device reported `maxLines=unlimited`, which is a fact about macOS on Apple Silicon and not about Windows or ALSA, and where a platform is less generous playback silently stops working after a while. And a `Clip` **holds the whole sound decoded**, so music means a multi-megabyte track resident with no way to fade, duck or crossfade except through per-line gain controls not every platform provides. Summing in software answers all three at once and makes per-bus volume, ducking and crossfade *arithmetic* rather than three more mechanisms.
  ⚠ **THE LINE IS THE CLOCK — there is no timer anywhere in the package.** Measured: writing 200 ms of frames into an 80 ms buffer took **162 ms**, because `SourceDataLine.write` blocks once the buffer is full. So the mix loop needs no sleep, no `Pulse`, no `Timeline` and no `AnimationTimer`, and the device's own consumption rate is a more accurate schedule than any clock this client could ask for. ⚠ That is also why the package needs **no exemption** from `UiContractTest`, which scans all of `src/main/java` for `AnimationTimer` and rations it to two files by name.
  ⚠ **A PLATFORM THREAD, DELIBERATELY NOT A VIRTUAL ONE** — the inverse of `presence/RichPresence`, which is right to use one. `write` blocks in a **native** call, and a virtual thread blocked in native code *pins* its carrier, so an audio engine on one quietly occupies a scheduler thread forever.
  ⚠ **THE BUS GAIN IS HANDED TO EACH VOICE PER BUFFER, NEVER BAKED IN AND NEVER APPLIED TO THE SUM.** The first implementation scaled the summed accumulator and was wrong outright: once music and effects are in one buffer there is no gain correct for both, so whichever is right leaves the other at the wrong level. Baking it into the voice at construction is the other tempting shape and breaks the case everybody actually tests — a volume change would apply only to sounds started *afterwards*, so dragging the music slider would do nothing to the music playing.
  ⚠ **A CUBIC SOFT CLIPPER CANNOT BE A TRANSPARENT LIMITER, and the first version here claimed to be.** `y = 1.5x − 0.5x³` is smooth and flat-topped at ±1, and its derivative at zero is **1.5** — so it was quietly making the whole game 3.5 dB louder. The general result rules out the family: for `y = ax + bx³` through the origin, unit slope forces `a = 1`, and passing through (1,1) then forces `b = 0`, the identity. **No cubic is both transparent at the origin and saturating at ±1**, so a limiter that is transparent where it matters has to be piecewise. Caught on the test's first run.
  ⚠ **REDUCE MOTION MUST NOT SILENCE THE GAME**, and this inverts the client's instinct everywhere else. Decoration here rides `Pulse.animate` and therefore stops under WCAG 2.2.2 — applying that reasoning to audio would take away the one channel that reaches a player who is not looking at the screen, i.e. an accessibility setting that costs somebody their notifications. **Sound is not motion.** WCAG **1.4.2** (Audio Control) is what actually applies, and it is satisfied by the **music bus being its own slider**, which is also the reason music and effects are not one control.
  ⚠ **WAVE, AU AND AIFF ONLY** — measured with `AudioSystem.getAudioFileTypes()`; the JDK ships no MP3 or Vorbis decoder. Resampling *is* provided and was verified on both JDKs on this machine (11 kHz mono → 44.1 kHz stereo, duration exact) — ⚠ unlike **secp256k1**, which `protocol/crypto` records as behaving *differently* on those same two runtimes. ⚠ **Format support is an SPI question, not a code one**: nothing in the package names a format, so a Vorbis provider on the classpath would load `.ogg` through the existing path unchanged. That matters because uncompressed music is **2.6 MB per minute** at 22 kHz mono and this client ships **five uber jars plus a jpackage image** — every megabyte is six of release. Left open as **AU-1**.
  ⚠ **FIVE OF THE SIX EFFECTS ARE SYNTHESISED**, the same decision the client already makes about its cursor, chrome, icons and wallpaper (§9 bans an icon set outright and every mark on screen is geometry). Zero bytes in six build outputs. ⚠ **Generation is deterministic and per-play pitch variation is not**, and the split is deliberate: a generated asset differing per run would mean two players hearing different games, while the pitch spread is safe because **nothing derived from it reaches a rule**.
  ⚠ **DECLARED IS NOT WIRED, on purpose.** Only `MESSAGE` has call sites. Deciding that a refusal makes a noise is an attention-ladder decision per `../client/05` §6, not a plumbing one, and it belongs to whoever owns each surface. **AU-2**.

- 2026-08-05 — **The operator panel dropped out of the strip onto the rail, because `Anchoring` right-aligned every overlay** — reported as "the profile identity looks like it's off screen a bit". `client/ui/{Anchoring,SyncBanner}`, `DeckShell`.

  ⚠ **"Right-align to the cell, clamp at zero" was correct until it was not, and the clamp is what hid it.** Every drop-down before the operator panel hung off a readout near the right-hand *end* of the strip, where growing leftward is exactly right. The operator cell is the **first** cell: right-aligning a 420px panel to a cell whose right edge is at 290 asks for **−130**, the clamp turned that into **0**, and what landed was a panel flush with the window edge, **over the rail**, aligned to nothing. ⚠ The clamp is why it looked like a rendering fault rather than a mistake — it produced a plausible number from an impossible one, so nothing anywhere reported a problem.
  ⚠ **The alignment is chosen rather than assumed now**: right-aligned when there is room to the left of the cell, **left-aligned to the cell** when there is not.
  ⚠ **`within` is THE DESK, never the deck root.** The rail is part of the root, so a clamp against the root cannot express "do not cover the rail" — it is the thing being clamped on top of. Both drawers pass `desk.root()`; the chain-sync report's version of this failure was **latent rather than absent**, since a narrow deck puts its left edge over the rail too.
  ⚠ **`min` before `max`.** An overlay wider than the desk satisfies neither bound, and the order decides which way it spills: right, where it stays readable from its first character, rather than left under the rail.
  ⚠ **The field is watched in its own right** — the rail collapses below `NARROW_WIDTH`, so the desk's left edge moves while the root's bounds do not change at all, and an overlay placed against the old edge would sit wrong until something else happened to move.
  ⚠ **The rule was extracted as pure package-private `Anchoring.horizontal` so it can be tested without a toolkit** — the seam `SecurityCenterView.latestOf` exists for, and for its reason: this shipped wrong *because* it lived inside a method that needs live scene bounds, so the only way to check it was to render the deck and look. `AnchoringTest` pins five cases and was verified against the clamp-only version first.

- 2026-08-05 — **Discord rich presence, opt-in and off by default; `../client/00` §7's "not a telemetry client" non-goal amended rather than stretched** — on explicit direction. `client/presence/{PresenceState,DiscordIpc,RichPresence,Transport}`, `ClientProfile.Settings.discordPresenceEnabled`, Settings → Discord. [`../client/00-client-overview.md` §7](../client/00-client-overview.md), [`../client/02-platform-native-themes.md` §2.9a](../client/02-platform-native-themes.md).

  ⚠ **THE CONTRACT CONFLICT IS REAL AND IS THE WHOLE DECISION.** Two clauses stood against this: §7's non-goal, and §2.9's exhaustive host-read list ending "**Never transmits:** any of the above". The second turned out not to bind — presence carries none of the host-read facts — and the first was **narrowed to what it protects rather than widened to permit an exception**: it governs *collection*, the game gathering facts and sending them somewhere the player did not choose, and every clause of that inverts for an opt-in broadcast to a program the player already runs on their own account. §2.9 gained a sibling **§2.9a** listing everything outbound that is not a home server, which is **two entries** — this and AnonShare's quote feed, whose shape was the precedent. Adding a third is now a documented decision.

  ⚠ **THE PRIVACY GUARANTEE IS STRUCTURAL, NOT DILIGENCE AT THE CALL SITES.** `RichPresence.activity` takes a `PresenceState` and an `Instant`; it is handed no session and has nothing in scope to read one from, so the set of things it can transmit *is* the set of constants in `PresenceState`. A format string would have been one interpolation away from a handle or a target address with nothing on screen reporting it — which is the failure mode that matters here, because the leak goes to a friends list and a third party rather than to a log the player can read.
  ⚠ **The one subject that could have smuggled something is the window id**: the desk publishes `shell:<address>` for a shell session, so a plausible "put the subject in the details line" edit ships a machine address to everyone the player knows. `PresenceState.forWindowId` resolves it to `TERMINAL` and drops the address, and that specific case has its own test.
  ⚠ **Verified against a deliberately-leaking build before being trusted** — three planted leaks (an address in a constant, a probe handle in the asset text, a `forWindowId` falling through to `DECK`), each caught by its intended assertion, then reverted.

  ⚠ **NO NEW DEPENDENCY, and that is worth more here than usual.** Discord RPC is local IPC — a named pipe on Windows, a Unix domain socket elsewhere — with an 8-byte little-endian header and UTF-8 JSON, all reachable from the JDK (`SocketChannel` + `StandardProtocolFamily.UNIX`, `RandomAccessFile`), and Jackson 3 was already on the client classpath. An RPC library would have meant widening `client/pom.xml`'s enforcer, a jar in all five platform uber jars, and fresh supply-chain surface on a repo that publishes **unsigned** executables — for two hundred bytes down a pipe.
  ⚠ **Little-endian is the one interesting thing about the frame and it fails silently when wrong**: the connection opens, the handshake is accepted, and Discord then waits for a payload sixteen million bytes long. Asserted byte by byte rather than through the reader, which would agree with its own mistake.
  ⚠ **Every reply is drained**, or the pipe buffer fills and the next write blocks forever — a feature that works for a few minutes and then stops with nothing logged.
  ⚠ **The frame length is bounded** (`MAX_FRAME_BYTES`) because it is chosen by another process and fed to `new byte[n]`.

  ⚠ **ONE UPDATE PER 15s, COALESCED — Discord's limit, not a preference.** The worker holds the latest wanted state rather than a queue; a queue would replay a stale sequence minutes behind the player. That is also what makes reporting a window *closing* as `DECK` safe: the focus event that follows overwrites it long before anything is transmitted.
  ⚠ **Two clocks, and it is the documented inversion of the session-clock rule.** Pacing uses `System.nanoTime` (how long *this machine* took — `ui/chrome/Frost`'s reasoning); the elapsed stamp uses `Instant.now()`, because Discord renders it as real time in front of another person and a test clock would tell somebody's friends the player started in 2026. Neither is a game deadline.
  ⚠ **Switching it off clears the activity rather than merely stopping updates**, and so does shutdown. A presence frozen on "At a terminal" after the player quit is the feature still talking about them.

  ⚠ **A blank application id is a SUPPORTED STATE, not a missing step.** The id is public — it identifies the game to Discord and is not a credential — but it belongs to whoever registered the application, so a fork and a contributor's local build have none. Blank disables the feature with one `FINE` line, the Settings switch is disabled and **says which of the three conditions applies** (no id / off / on but Discord absent), and the whole suite passes. It arrives through `build.properties`, the one Maven-filtered resource, with `-Deyeandsickle.discord.appId=` overriding at run time.

  ⚠ **The vocabulary caption is GENERATED from the enum, and the first version was unreadable.** A hand-typed copy would be true the day it was written and would then become a false statement about what the client transmits — the worst thing for the caption a player reads to decide whether to consent. Rendering also showed that joining sixteen states with separators produced four lines of run-on prose 1300px wide: correct, wrapped properly, and the wrong shape for something whose job is to be *audited*. One per line. **Found by rendering**; `DeckSnapshot` gained `-Ddeck.settingsPage=` because the panel opens on its first category and every other page was otherwise unrenderable.

  ⚠ **The presence strings are deliberately NOT translated.** The reader is not the player — it is whoever is on their friends list — and the language a player set their own interface to says nothing about what those people read. Same line `../client/04` draws between structure and prose for command names.

  ⚠ **`applicationId()` had to become an instance method with a test seam, and the reason is worth keeping.** As a static reading only the property and the resource, a test could express "no id" only by clearing the system property — which is "resolve normally", so it found whatever `<discord.app.id>` had put in `build.properties`. It therefore passed exactly while the pom was empty and failed on the first build that set a real id, which is the first build where any of this mattered. **A test that is green because a build property happens to be unconfigured is testing the build, not the code.** ⚠ And the red build was the lesser half: with the built-in id satisfying the check, that test started the worker holding the **real** connector and would have opened a pipe to the developer's own Discord — the exact side effect `DiscordIpcTest` avoids by never calling `connect`. Fixed with `useApplicationId("")`, a recording transport installed for **every** test in the file, and a partner test asserting a configured id *does* start, since a refusal test with no partner passes equally well when the thing is unreachable for an unrelated reason.

  **Open:** the id and the `uos` art asset are registered out-of-band and are not in the repo.

- 2026-08-05 — **§9's ban on glassmorphism amended to an opt-in: uOS Modern Liquid Abs, dark and light** — on explicit direction, by the same mechanism and under the same four conditions as §9.1 (artefacts), §9.2 (casing) and §9.3 (rounded corners). [`ui-design-language.md` §9.4](ui-design-language.md); `ThemeId.{LIQUID_DARK,LIQUID_LIGHT}`, `theme-liquid-{dark,light}.css`. **Drop shadows and blur are NOT included and remain build-blocking.**
  - ⚠ **THE BACKDROP BLUR IS REAL, AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG ABOUT THAT.** It recorded, as a measured fact, that "a real frosted pane is not expressible in this toolkit at any price". That is true of **CSS** — there is no `backdrop-filter`, and `-fx-effect: gaussianblur(...)` blurs a node's *own* content — and it is not true of the toolkit. `ui/chrome/Frost` (2026-08-05) snapshots what is beneath each window, blurs the image and paints it under the panel. ⚠ **§9's ban is untouched**: it objects to blur and shadow applied to *the interface*, which softens the edges the whole geometry budget exists to keep hard; blurring a **picture of what is behind** a window leaves every hairline and glyph exactly as sharp, and the CSS scan still runs across all eight stylesheets.
  - ⚠ **Three decisions make it affordable.** The capture is the **whole desk** rather than the window's rectangle, so a backdrop changes only when the content behind it changes and **dragging is free and pixel-accurate**; it is taken at **0.4 scale**, because a blur discards high frequencies by definition (and JavaFX caps `GaussianBlur` at 63, which is a cap on the *downscaled* radius, not the visible one); and it is **one capture per window, bottom-up**, so frame *n* sees exactly frames *0..n-1*. ⚠ It is **deferred and coalesced** — `snapshot` forces a CSS and layout pass, so calling it from inside one re-enters layout — and `DeskManager.frostNow()` exists solely because **no queued runnable executes during a synchronous `Scene.snapshot`**, so a render harness on the normal path photographs glass windows with nothing behind them.
  - ⚠ **IT REFRESHES AT UP TO 24 FPS ON ITS OWN CLOCK** (`UiTokens.FROST_MS`), not `Pulse` — which ticks at 100ms and quantises every subscriber to a multiple of it, so a request for 24fps rounds silently to 10. ⚠ **The measurements are the design**: four windows at 1600×1000, one capture *per window* costs **~40ms** (a 24fps *ceiling*, i.e. the whole thread); each cut to its own window's rectangle, **~37ms**; **one shared capture, ~9ms**. The middle number is the counter-intuitive finding — **`snapshot` renders the whole node whatever the viewport says, and the viewport only crops the result** — so cost is the *number* of snapshots and barely their size.
